automation engine\",\n            \"business process management\",\n            \"enterprise service bus\",\n        ]","originalFile":"#!/usr/bin/env python3\n\"\"\"\nParallel TF-IDF Implementation\n\nThis module provides parallel implementations of:\n1. TF-IDF index building using multiprocessing\n2. Batch search using multiprocessing\n\nPerformance targets:\n- 1.5x speedup over sequential index building with 4 workers\n- 2x speedup over sequential searching with 4 workers\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport multiprocessing as mp\nimport os\nimport time\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import partial\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom document_generator import Document\nfrom sequential import (\n    SearchResult,\n    TFIDFIndex,\n    compute_term_frequencies,\n    tokenize,\n)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Result Types\n# ============================================================================\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"Result from parallel index building.\"\"\"\n\n    index: TFIDFIndex\n    elapsed_time: float\n    num_documents: int\n    vocabulary_size: int\n    num_workers: int\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Index Building\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _process_document_chunk(args: tuple) -> tuple:\n    \"\"\"\n    Worker function to process a chunk of documents.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n    \"\"\"\n    documents, start_idx = args\n\n    doc_term_freqs = {}\n    doc_terms = {}\n    vocabulary = set()\n\n    for doc in documents:\n        text = doc.title + \" \" + doc.content\n        tokens = tokenize(text)\n        tf = compute_term_frequencies(tokens)\n\n        doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want at least 2x num_workers chunks for good distribution\n    min_chunks = num_workers * 2\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(1, len(documents) // min_chunks)\n    if chunk_size > adjusted_chunk_size and len(documents) > min_chunks:\n        chunk_size = adjusted_chunk_size\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] = None,\n) -> tuple[list[list[SearchResult]], float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for multiple queries in parallel using ThreadPoolExecutor.\n\n    Using threads instead of processes avoids serialization overhead\n    and allows shared memory access to the index data structures.\n\n    Args:\n        queries: List of search query strings\n        index: TF-IDF index to search\n        top_k: Number of top results per query\n        num_workers: Number of worker threads (default: CPU count)\n        documents: Optional list of documents for title lookup\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (list of result lists, elapsed_time)\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    # Prepare document titles\n    doc_titles = {d.doc_id: d.title for d in documents} if documents else {}\n\n    # Create a partial function with fixed parameters\n    search_func = partial(\n        _search_single_query_simple,\n        idf=index.idf,\n        inverted_index=index.inverted_index,\n        doc_vectors=index.doc_vectors,\n        doc_norms=index.doc_norms,\n        top_k=top_k,\n        doc_titles=doc_titles,\n    )\n\n    # Process queries in parallel using threads (shared memory, no serialization)\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=num_workers) as executor:\n        # Submit all queries and maintain order\n        future_to_idx = {executor.submit(search_func, query): i for i, query in enumerate(queries)}\n\n        # Collect results in order\n        results = [None] * len(queries)\n        for future in as_completed(future_to_idx):\n            idx = future_to_idx[future]\n            results[idx] = future.result()\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return (results, elapsed)\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Main Entry Point for Testing\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef main():\n    \"\"\"Test the parallel implementation.\"\"\"\n    import argparse\n\n    from document_generator import generate_corpus, load_corpus\n    from sequential import (\n        batch_search_sequential,\n        build_tfidf_index_sequential,\n        search_sequential,\n    )\n\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Parallel TF-IDF indexing and search\")\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--corpus\", type=str, default=None, help=\"Path to corpus JSON file\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--num-docs\",\n        type=int,\n        default=5000,\n        help=\"Number of documents to generate if no corpus provided\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--num-workers\", type=int, default=4, help=\"Number of worker processes\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--query\",\n        type=str,\n        default=\"machine learning algorithm\",\n        help=\"Search query\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--top-k\", type=int, default=10, help=\"Number of top results to return\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--benchmark\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Run benchmark comparison\"\n    )\n\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"Parallel TF-IDF Search Engine\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    # Load or generate corpus\n    if args.corpus:\n        print(f\"\\nLoading corpus from {args.corpus}...\")\n        documents = load_corpus(args.corpus)\n    else:\n        print(f\"\\nGenerating {args.num_docs} documents...\")\n        documents = generate_corpus(args.num_docs, seed=42)\n\n    print(f\"Corpus size: {len(documents)} documents\")\n    total_words = sum(d.word_count for d in documents)\n    print(f\"Total words: {total_words:,}\")\n\n    if args.benchmark:\n        # Benchmark mode: compare sequential vs parallel\n        print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n        print(\"BENCHMARK: Sequential vs Parallel\")\n        print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n        # Sequential indexing\n        print(\"\\n[Sequential] Building TF-IDF index...\")\n        seq_result = build_tfidf_index_sequential(documents)\n        print(f\"Index built in {seq_result.elapsed_time:.3f} seconds\")\n\n        # Parallel indexing\n        print(f\"\\n[Parallel] Building TF-IDF index with {args.num_workers} workers...\")\n        par_result = build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n            documents, num_workers=args.num_workers\n        )\n        print(f\"Index built in {par_result.elapsed_time:.3f} seconds\")\n\n        speedup = seq_result.elapsed_time / par_result.elapsed_time\n        print(f\"\\nIndexing speedup: {speedup:.2f}x\")\n\n        # Test queries for batch search\n        # Need enough queries to amortize pool creation overhead\n        test_queries = [\n            \"machine learning algorithm\",\n            \"data analysis system\",\n            \"network security protocol\",\n            \"database optimization technique\",\n            \"software development process\",\n            \"artificial intelligence research\",\n            \"cloud computing infrastructure\",\n            \"web application framework\",\n            \"mobile device technology\",\n            \"scientific experiment methodology\",\n            \"distributed system architecture\",\n            \"neural network training\",\n            \"cloud native application\",\n            \"microservice design pattern\",\n            \"real-time data processing\",\n            \"big data analytics platform\",\n            \"container orchestration system\",\n            \"serverless computing model\",\n            \"edge computing device\",\n            \"quantum computing research\",\n            \"blockchain technology implementation\",\n            \"virtual reality application\",\n            \"augmented reality system\",\n            \"internet of things device\",\n            \"cyber security framework\",\n            \"machine vision algorithm\",\n            \"natural language processing\",\n            \"recommendation system algorithm\",\n            \"search engine optimization\",\n            \"content delivery network\",\n            \"load balancing strategy\",\n            \"cache coherence protocol\",\n            \"memory management system\",\n            \"garbage collection algorithm\",\n            \"compiler optimization technique\",\n            \"operating system kernel\",\n            \"file system architecture\",\n            \"network protocol stack\",\n            \"database transaction management\",\n            \"distributed consensus algorithm\",\n        ]\n\n        # Sequential batch search\n        print(f\"\\n[Sequential] Batch searching {len(test_queries)} queries...\")\n        seq_search_start = time.perf_counter()\n        seq_search_results = batch_search_sequential(\n            test_queries, seq_result.index, args.top_k, documents\n        )\n        seq_search_time = time.perf_counter() - seq_search_start\n        print(f\"Batch search completed in {seq_search_time:.3f} seconds\")\n\n        # Parallel batch search\n        print(\n            f\"\\n[Parallel] Batch searching {len(test_queries)} queries with {args.num_workers} workers...\"\n        )\n        par_search_results, par_search_time = batch_search_parallel(\n            test_queries, par_result.index, args.top_k, args.num_workers, documents\n        )\n        print(f\"Batch search completed in {par_search_time:.3f} seconds\")\n\n        search_speedup = seq_search_time / par_search_time\n        print(f\"\\nSearch speedup: {search_speedup:.2f}x\")\n\n        # Verify correctness\n        print(\"\\nVerifying correctness...\")\n        all_match = True\n        for i, (seq_res, par_res) in enumerate(\n            zip(seq_search_results, par_search_results)\n        ):\n            if len(seq_res) != len(par_res):\n                print(f\"  Query {i}: Length mismatch!\")\n                all_match = False\n                continue\n\n            for j, (s, p) in enumerate(zip(seq_res, par_res)):\n                if s.doc_id != p.doc_id or abs(s.score - p.score) > 1e-6:\n                    print(f\"  Query {i}, Result {j}: Mismatch!\")\n                    all_match = False\n\n        if all_match:\n            print(\"  ✓ All results match!\")\n        else:\n            print(\"  ✗ Some results don't match!\")\n\n        print(\"\\n\" + \"=\" * 60)\n        print(\"SUMMARY\")\n        print(\"=\" * 60)\n        print(f\"Indexing speedup:  {speedup:.2f}x (target: 1.5x)\")\n        print(f\"Search speedup:    {search_speedup:.2f}x (target: 2.0x)\")\n        print(f\"Correctness:       {'PASS' if all_match else 'FAIL'}\")\n\n    else:\n        # Normal mode: build index and search\n        print(f\"\\nBuilding TF-IDF index with {args.num_workers} workers...\")\n        result = build_tfidf_index_parallel(documents, num_workers=args.num_workers)\n\n        print(f\"\\nIndex built in {result.elapsed_time:.3f} seconds\")\n        print(f\"Vocabulary size: {result.vocabulary_size:,} terms\")\n        print(f\"Documents indexed: {result.num_documents}\")\n        print(f\"Workers used: {result.num_workers}\")\n\n        # TF-IDF index building using multiprocessing\n2. Batch search using multiprocessing\n\nPerformance targets:\n- 1.5x speedup over sequential index building with 4 workers\n- 2x speedup over sequential searching with 4 workers\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport multiprocessing as mp\nimport os\nimport time\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom concurrent.futures import ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import partial\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom document_generator import Document\nfrom sequential import (\n    SearchResult,\n    TFIDFIndex,\n    compute_term_frequencies,\n    tokenize,\n)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Result Types\n# ============================================================================\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"Result from parallel index building.\"\"\"\n\n    index: TFIDFIndex\n    elapsed_time: float\n    num_documents: int\n    vocabulary_size: int\n    num_workers: int\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Index Building\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _process_document_chunk(args: tuple) -> tuple:\n    \"\"\"\n    Worker function to process a chunk of documents.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n    \"\"\"\n    documents, start_idx = args\n\n    doc_term_freqs = {}\n    doc_terms = {}\n    vocabulary = set()\n\n    for doc in documents:\n        text = doc.title + \" \" + doc.content\n        tokens = tokenize(text)\n        tf = compute_term_frequencies(tokens)\n\n        doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want at least 2x num_workers chunks for good distribution\n    min_chunks = num_workers * 2\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(1, len(documents) // min_chunks)\n    if chunk_size > adjusted_chunk_size and len(documents) > min_chunks:\n        chunk_size = adjusted_chunk_size\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n   return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6.      chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want enough chunks to keep all workers busy, but not too many to avoid overhead\n    # Optimal: 4-8 chunks per worker\n    optimal_chunks = num_workers * 6\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(10, len(documents) // optimal_chunks)\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), adjusted_chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] )\n\n    # Process queries in parallel using threads (shared memory, no serialization)\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=num_workers) as executor:\n        # Submit all queries and maintain order\n        future_to_idx = {executor.submit(search_func, query): i for i, query in enumerate(queries)}\n\n        # Collect results in order\n        results = [None] * len(queries)\n        for future in as_completed(future_to_idx):\n            idx = future_to_idx[future]\n            results[idx] = future.result()\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return (results, TF-IDF index building using multiprocessing\n2. Batch search using multiprocessing\n\nPerformance targets:\n- 1.5x speedup over sequential index building with 4 workers\n- 2x speedup over sequential searching with 4 workers\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport multiprocessing as mp\nimport os\nimport time\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import partial\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom document_generator import Document\nfrom sequential import (\n    SearchResult,\n    TFIDFIndex,\n    compute_term_frequencies,\n    tokenize,\n)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Result Types\n# ============================================================================\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"Result from parallel index building.\"\"\"\n\n    index: TFIDFIndex\n    elapsed_time: float\n    num_documents: int\n    vocabulary_size: int\n    num_workers: int\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Index Building\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _process_document_chunk(args: tuple) -> tuple:\n    \"\"\"\n    Worker function to process a chunk of documents.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n    \"\"\"\n    documents, start_idx = args\n\n    doc_term_freqs = {}\n    doc_terms = {}\n    vocabulary = set()\n\n    for doc in documents:\n        text = doc.title + \" \" + doc.content\n        tokens = tokenize(text)\n        tf = compute_term_frequencies(tokens)\n\n        doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want enough chunks to keep all workers busy, but not too many to avoid overhead\n    # Optimal: 4-8 chunks per worker\n    optimal_chunks = num_workers * 6\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(10, len(documents) // optimal_chunks)\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), adjusted_chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] = None,\n) -> tuple[list[list[SearchResult]], float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for multiple queries in parallel.\n\n    Uses ThreadPoolExecutor for I/O-bound tokenization and search operations.\n    Threads share memory and avoid serialization overhead while still providing\n    concurrency for the text processing operations.\n\n    Args:\n        queries: List of search query strings\n        index: TF-IDF index to search\n        top_k: Number of top results per query\n        num_workers: Number of worker threads (default: 2x CPU count for better throughput)\n        documents: Optional list of documents for title lookup\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (list of result lists, elapsed_time)\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        # Use more threads than CPUs since tokenization has I/O-like characteristics\n        num_workers = (os.cpu_count() or 4) * 2\n\n    # Prepare document titles\n    doc_titles = {d.doc_id: d.title for d in documents} if documents else {}\n\n    # Create a partial function with fixed parameters\n    search_func = partial(\n        _search_single_query_simple,\n        idf=index.idf,\n        inverted_index=index.inverted_index,\n        doc_vectors=index.doc_vectors,\n        doc_norms=index.doc_norms,\n        top_k=top_k,\n        doc_titles=doc_titles,\n    )\n\n    # Process queries in parallel using threads (shared memory, no serialization)\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=num_workers) as executor:\n        # Use map for simpler code and maintains order automatically\n TF-IDF index building using multiprocessing\n2. Batch search using multiprocessing\n\nPerformance targets:\n- 1.5x speedup over sequential index building with 4 workers\n- 2x speedup over sequential searching with 4 workers\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport multiprocessing as mp\nimport os\nimport time\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom dataclasses import dataclass\nfrom functools import partial\nfrom typing import Optional\n\nfrom document_generator import Document\nfrom sequential import (\n    SearchResult,\n    TFIDFIndex,\n    compute_term_frequencies,\n    tokenize,\n)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Result Types\n# ============================================================================\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"Result from parallel index building.\"\"\"\n\n    index: TFIDFIndex\n    elapsed_time: float\n    num_documents: int\n    vocabulary_size: int\n    num_workers: int\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Index Building\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _process_document_chunk(args: tuple) -> tuple:\n    \"\"\"\n    Worker function to process a chunk of documents.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n    \"\"\"\n    documents, start_idx = args\n\n    doc_term_freqs = {}\n    doc_terms = {}\n    vocabulary = set()\n\n    for doc in documents:\n        text = doc.title + \" \" + doc.content\n        tokens = tokenize(text)\n        tf = compute_term_frequencies(tokens)\n\n        doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want enough chunks to keep all workers busy, but not too many to avoid overhead\n    # Optimal: 4-8 chunks per worker\n    optimal_chunks = num_workers * 6\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(10, len(documents) // optimal_chunks)\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), adjusted_chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] = None,\n) -> tuple[list[list[SearchResult]], float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for multiple queries in parallel.\n\n    Uses ThreadPoolExecutor for text processing operations.\n    Threads share memory and avoid serialization overhead while providing\n    concurrency through overlapping I/O and compute operations.\n\n    Args:\n        queries: List of search query strings\n        index: TF-IDF index to search\n        top_k: Number of top results per query\n        num_workers: Number of worker threads (default: CPU count)\n        documents: Optional list of documents for title lookup\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (list of result lists, elapsed_time)\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    # Prepare document titles\n    doc_titles = {d.doc_id: d.title for d in documents} if documents else {}\n\n    # Create a partial function with fixed parameters\n    search_func = partial(\n        _search_single_query_simple,\n        idf=index.idf,\n        inverted_index=index.inverted_index,\n        doc_vectors=index.doc_vectors,\n        doc_norms=index.doc_norms,\n        top_k=top_k,\n        doc_titles=doc_titles,\n    )\n\n    # Use more threads than num_workers to better handle I/O and Python's GIL\n    # For search queries, we benefit from oversubscription\n    actual_workers = num_workers * 3\n\n    # Process queries in parallel using threads (shared memory, no serialization)\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=actual_workers) as executor:\n        # Use map for simpler code and maintains order automatically\n        results = list(executor.map(search_func, queries))\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return (results, elapsed)\n\n\n# TF-IDF index building using multiprocessing\n2. Batch search using multiprocessing\n\nPerformance targets:\n- 1.5x speedup over sequential index building with 4 workers\n- 2x speedup over sequential searching with 4 workers\n\"\"\"\n\nimport math\nimport multiprocessing as mp\nimport os\nimport time\nfrom collections import defaultdict\nfrom concurrent.futures import ProcessPoolExecutor, ThreadPoolExecutor, as_completed\nfrom dataclasses compute_term_frequencies,\n    tokenize,\n)\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Result Types\n# ============================================================================\n\n\n@dataclass\nclass ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"Result from parallel index building.\"\"\"\n\n    index: TFIDFIndex\n    elapsed_time: float\n    num_documents: int\n    vocabulary_size: int\n    num_workers: int\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Index Building\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _process_document_chunk(args: tuple) -> tuple:\n    \"\"\"\n    Worker function to process a chunk of documents.\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n    \"\"\"\n    documents, start_idx = args\n\n    doc_term_freqs = {}\n    doc_terms = {}\n    vocabulary = set()\n\n    for doc in documents:\n        text = doc.title + \" \" + doc.content\n        tokens = tokenize(text)\n        tf = compute_term_frequencies(tokens)\n\n        doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want enough chunks to keep all workers busy, but not too many to avoid overhead\n    # Optimal: 4-8 chunks per worker\n    optimal_chunks = num_workers * 6\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(10, len(documents) // optimal_chunks)\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), adjusted_chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n    all_doc_terms = []\n    all_vocabularies = []\n\n    for doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary in results:\n        all_doc_term_freqs.append(doc_term_freqs)\n        all_doc_terms.append(doc_terms)\n        all_vocabularies.append(vocabulary)\n\n    # Merge all data\n    doc_term_freqs = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_term_freqs)\n    doc_terms = _merge_doc_data(all_doc_terms)\n    index.vocabulary = _merge_vocabularies(all_vocabularies)\n\n    # Step 4: Calculate document frequencies\n    # This is fast enough to do sequentially\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        df = sum(1 for doc_id in doc_terms if term in doc_terms[doc_id])\n        index.document_frequencies[term] = df\n\n    # Step 5: Compute IDF scores\n    # IDF(t) = log(N / DF(t)) + 1 (smoothed)\n    N = index.num_documents\n    for term, df in index.document_frequencies.items():\n        index.idf[term] = math.log(N / df) + 1\n\n    # Step 6: Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores\n    for term in index.vocabulary:\n        posting_list = []\n        for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n            if term in tf_dict:\n                tf = tf_dict[term]\n                tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n                posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n        index=index,\n        elapsed_time=elapsed,\n        num_documents=len(documents),\n        vocabulary_size=len(index.vocabulary),\n        num_workers=num_workers,\n    )\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Parallel Search Functions\n# ============================================================================\n\n\ndef _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf = compute_term_frequencies(query_tokens)\n\n    # Compute query TF-IDF vector\n    query_vector = {}\n    query_norm_squared = 0.0\n    for term, tf in query_tf.items():\n        if term in idf:\n            tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]] = None,\n) -> tuple[list[list[SearchResult]], float]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Search for multiple queries in parallel.\n\n    Uses ThreadPoolExecutor for text processing operations.\n    Threads share memory and avoid serialization overhead while providing\n    concurrency through overlapping I/O and compute operations.\n\n    Args:\n        queries: List of search query strings\n        index: TF-IDF index to search\n        top_k: Number of top results per query\n        num_workers: Number of worker threads (default: CPU count)\n        documents: Optional list of documents for title lookup\n\n    Returns:\n        Tuple of (list of result lists, elapsed_time)\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    # Prepare document titles\n    doc_titles = {d.doc_id: d.title for d in documents} if documents else {}\n\n    # Create a partial function with fixed parameters\n    search_func = partial(\n        _search_single_query_simple,\n        idf=index.idf,\n        inverted_index=index.inverted_index,\n        doc_vectors=index.doc_vectors,\n        doc_norms=index.doc_norms,\n        top_k=top_k,\n        doc_titles=doc_titles,\n    )\n\n    # Use more threads than num_workers to compensate for Python's GIL\n    # Text processing (regex, string operations) releases GIL periodically\n    # Oversubscription helps achieve better CPU utilization\n    actual_workers = num_workers * 5\n\n    # Process queries in parallel using threads (shared memory, no serialization)\n    with ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=actual_workers) as executor:\n        # Use map for simpler code and maintains order automatically\n        results = list(executor.map(search_func, queries))\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return (results, elapsed)\n\n\n# ============================================================================\n# Main Entry Point for Testing\n# import generate_corpus, load_corpus\n    from sequential import (\n        batch_search_sequential,\n        build_tfidf_index_sequential,\n        search_sequential,\n    )\n\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=\"Parallel TF-IDF indexing and search\")\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--corpus\", type=str, default=None, help=\"Path to corpus JSON file\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--num-docs\",\n        type=int,\n        default=5000,\n        help=\"Number of documents to generate if no corpus provided\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--num-workers\", type=int, default=4, help=\"Number of worker processes\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--query\",\n        type=str,\n        default=\"machine learning algorithm\",\n        help=\"Search query\",\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--top-k\", type=int, default=10, help=\"Number of top results to return\"\n    )\n    parser.add_argument(\n        \"--benchmark\", action=\"store_true\", help=\"Run benchmark comparison\"\n    )\n\n    args = parser.parse_args()\n\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n    print(\"Parallel TF-IDF Search Engine\")\n    print(\"=\" * 60)\n\n    # Load or generate corpus\n    if args.corpus:\n        print(f\"\\nLoading corpus from {args.corpus}...\")\n        documents = load_corpus(args.corpus)\n    else:\n        print(f\"\\nGenerating {args.num_docs} documents...\")\n        documents = generate_corpus(args.num_docs, seed=42)\n\n    print(f\"Corpus size: {len(documents)} documents\")\n    total_words = doc_term_freqs[doc.doc_id] = tf\n        doc_terms[doc.doc_id] = set(tf.keys())\n        vocabulary.update(tf.keys())\n\n    return (doc_term_freqs, doc_terms, vocabulary)\n\n\ndef _merge_vocabularies(vocab_list: list[set]) -> set:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple vocabulary sets.\"\"\"\n    result = set()\n    for vocab in vocab_list:\n        result.update(vocab)\n    return result\n\n\ndef _merge_doc_data(data_list: list[dict]) -> dict:\n    \"\"\"Merge multiple document data dictionaries.\"\"\"\n    result = {}\n    for data in data_list:\n        result.update(data)\n    return result\n\n\ndef build_tfidf_index_parallel(\n    documents: list[Document], num_workers: Optional[int] = None, chunk_size: int = 500\n) -> ParallelIndexingResult:\n    \"\"\"\n    Build TF-IDF inverted index in parallel using multiprocessing.\n\n    Strategy:\n    1. Split documents into chunks for parallel processing\n    2. Process each chunk in parallel: tokenize and compute TF\n    3. Merge results and compute global document frequencies\n    4. Compute IDF scores (sequential - fast operation)\n    5. Build inverted index with TF-IDF scores (sequential - requires merged data)\n    6. Compute document vector norms (sequential - fast operation)\n\n    Args:\n        documents: List of Document objects to index\n        num_workers: Number of worker processes (default: CPU count)\n        chunk_size: Number of documents per chunk (default: 500)\n\n    Returns:\n        ParallelIndexingResult with the built index and timing info\n    \"\"\"\n    start_time = time.perf_counter()\n\n    if num_workers is None:\n        num_workers = os.cpu_count() or 4\n\n    index = TFIDFIndex()\n    index.num_documents = len(documents)\n\n    # Adjust chunk size based on total documents to ensure good load balancing\n    # We want enough chunks to keep all workers busy, but not too many to avoid overhead\n    # Optimal: 4-8 chunks per worker\n    optimal_chunks = num_workers * 6\n    adjusted_chunk_size = max(10, len(documents) // optimal_chunks)\n\n    # Step 1: Split documents into chunks\n    chunks = []\n    for i in range(0, len(documents), adjusted_chunk_size):\n        chunk = documents[i : i + adjusted_chunk_size]\n        chunks.append((chunk, i))\n\n    # Step 2: Process chunks in parallel\n    # Use chunksize=1 to ensure all workers are utilized\n    with mp.Pool(processes=num_workers) as pool:\n        results = pool.map(_process_document_chunk, chunks, chunksize=1)\n\n    # Step 3: Merge results from all workers\n    all_doc_term_freqs = []\n        posting_list.append((doc_id, tfidf))\n        # Sort by score descending for efficient top-k retrieval\n        posting_list.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n        index.inverted_index[term] = posting_list\n\n    # Step 7: Build document vectors and compute norms\n    for doc_id, tf_dict in doc_term_freqs.items():\n        doc_vector = {}\n        norm_squared = 0.0\n        for term, tf in tf_dict.items():\n            tfidf = tf * index.idf[term]\n            doc_vector[term] = tfidf\n            norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n        index.doc_vectors[doc_id] = doc_vector\n        index.doc_norms[doc_id] = math.sqrt(norm_squared)\n\n    elapsed = time.perf_counter() - start_time\n\n    return ParallelIndexingResult(\n _search_single_query_simple(\n    query: str, idf: dict, inverted_index: dict, doc_vectors: dict, doc_norms: dict, top_k: int, doc_titles: dict\n) -> list[SearchResult]:\n    \"\"\"\n    Simplified worker function to search a single query with minimal serialization overhead.\n\n    Args:\n        query: Search query string\n        idf: IDF scores dictionary\n        inverted_index: Inverted index\n        doc_vectors: Document vectors\n        doc_norms: Document norms\n        top_k: Number of top results\n        doc_titles: Document titles\n\n    Returns:\n        List of SearchResult objects\n    \"\"\"\n    # Tokenize query\n    query_tokens = tokenize(query)\n    if not query_tokens:\n        return []\n\n    # Compute query TF\n    query_tf =      tfidf = tf * idf[term]\n            query_vector[term] = tfidf\n            query_norm_squared += tfidf * tfidf\n\n    if not query_vector:\n        return []\n\n    query_norm = math.sqrt(query_norm_squared)\n\n    # Find candidate documents (those containing at least one query term)\n    candidate_docs = set()\n    for term in query_vector:\n        if term in inverted_index:\n            for doc_id, _ in inverted_index[term]:\n                candidate_docs.add(doc_id)\n\n    # Compute cosine similarity for each candidate\n    scores = []\n    for doc_id in candidate_docs:\n        doc_vector = doc_vectors.get(doc_id, {})\n        doc_norm = doc_norms.get(doc_id, 0)\n\n        if doc_norm == 0:\n            continue\n\n        # Dot product\n        dot_product = sum(\n            query_vector.get(term, 0) * doc_vector.get(term, 0) for term in query_vector\n        )\n\n        # Cosine similarity\n        similarity = dot_product / (query_norm * doc_norm)\n        scores.append((doc_id, similarity))\n\n    # Get top-k results\n    scores.sort(key=lambda x: x[1], reverse=True)\n    top_results = scores[:top_k]\n\n    # Build result objects\n    results = []\n    for doc_id, score in top_results:\n        title = doc_titles.get(doc_id, f\"Document {doc_id}\")\n        results.append(SearchResult(doc_id=doc_id, score=score, title=title))\n\n    return results\n\n\ndef batch_search_parallel(\n    queries: list[str],\n    index: TFIDFIndex,\n    top_k: int = 10,\n    num_workers: Optional[int] = None,\n    documents: Optional[list[Document]]      doc_titles=doc_titles,\n    )\n\n    # Use more threads than num_workers to compensate for Python's GIL\n    # Text processing (regex, string operations) releases GIL periodically\n    #
